output
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""RED for the right reason: the tester's new intent tests fail on the behaviour, not the wiring.
3	
4	Called by `scripts/check.sh collect` with the intent test files changed since the task's base.
5	Runs them once and sorts every test by why it ended:
6	
7	    right  AssertionError, NotImplementedError (an interface stub, `raise
8	           NotImplementedError("M1-T05")`) or pytest.fail / pytest.raises' "DID NOT RAISE"
9	    wrong  anything else: ImportError, AttributeError, NameError, TypeError, a fixture or
10	           collection error. The test can't reach the behaviour it claims to check.
11	    passed the code already does this; listed, not a failure
12	
13	Exits 1 when any test is wrong, or when none is red: a test that can't fail proves nothing.
14	With no changed test file there is nothing to judge, and it exits 0.
15	Prints one line per test that is not right, as `nodeid: reason`.
16	"""
17	
18	from __future__ import annotations
19	
20	import importlib
21	import sys
22	from typing import Any
23	
24	RIGHT = {"AssertionError", "NotImplementedError", "Failed"}  # Failed: pytest.fail, DID NOT RAISE
25	
26	
27	def _right(exc: BaseException | None) -> bool:
28	    return exc is not None and bool({c.__name__ for c in type(exc).__mro__} & RIGHT)
29	
30	
31	class Recorder:
32	    """A pytest plugin that keeps how each test ended: right, wrong (with why) or passed."""
33	
34	    def __init__(self) -> None:
35	        self.results: dict[str, str] = {}
36	
37	    def pytest_collectreport(self, report: Any) -> None:
38	        if report.failed:
39	            self.results[report.nodeid or "collection"] = "wrong: collection error"
40	
41	    def pytest_exception_interact(self, node: Any, call: Any, report: Any) -> None:
42	        if not hasattr(node, "function"):
43	            return  # a collector: pytest_collectreport has it
44	        exc = call.excinfo.value if call.excinfo else None
45	        if call.when == "call" and _right(exc):
46	            self.results[node.nodeid] = "right"
47	        else:
48	            self.results[node.nodeid] = f"wrong: {call.when}: {type(exc).__name__}"
49	
50	    def pytest_runtest_logreport(self, report: Any) -> None:
51	        if report.when == "call" and report.passed:
52	            self.results.setdefault(report.nodeid, "passed")
53	
54	
55	def main(paths: list[str]) -> int:
56	    if not paths:
57	        print("red_check: no intent test changed since the base; nothing to check", file=sys.stderr)
58	        return 0
59	    # pytest is a dev dependency, present wherever check.sh runs. Imported by name: deptry audits
60	    # the product's imports, and this script is the bar's.
61	    pytest = importlib.import_module("pytest")
62	    recorder = Recorder()
63	    # -m "": every marker, so a slow or perf intent test the default addopts deselect is judged too.
64	    pytest.main(
65	        [*paths, "-q", "--no-header", "--no-cov", "-m", "", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"], [recorder]
66	    )
67	    results = recorder.results
68	    wrong = {n: r for n, r in results.items() if r.startswith("wrong")}
69	    for nodeid, why in sorted(wrong.items()):
70	        print(f"{nodeid}: fails for the wrong reason ({why.removeprefix('wrong: ')})")
71	    for nodeid in sorted(n for n, r in results.items() if r == "passed"):
72	        print(f"{nodeid}: passes already (not red)")
73	    if wrong:
74	        print("red_check: fix the tests above: each must reach the code and fail on its behaviour")
75	        return 1
76	    red = sum(r == "right" for r in results.values())
77	    if not red:
78	        print("red_check: no test is red; a test that can't fail proves nothing")
79	        return 1
80	    print(f"red_check: {red} red for the right reason", file=sys.stderr)
81	    return 0
82	
83	
84	if __name__ == "__main__":
85	    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
86

"""DATA-02: unusable rows are dropped and duplicates resolved (TC-01)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import RawRow, validate_rows

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def test_tc01_unusable_rows_and_duplicates(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-01")
    rows = [RawRow(date, ticker, close) for date, ticker, close in given["rows"]]
    out = validate_rows(rows, given["tickers"])
    assert [[r.date, r.ticker, r.close] for r in out] == expected["rows"]


def test_requested_ticker_with_no_usable_row_is_key_error():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 10.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "B", None),
        RawRow("2024-01-03", "B", 0.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-04", "B", -1.0),
    ]
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="B"):
        validate_rows(rows, ["A", "B"])


def test_requested_ticker_absent_from_the_file_is_key_error():
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="Z"):
        validate_rows([RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 10.0)], ["Z"])

"""PL-01 step 2: keep the usable rows, sorted."""

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import RawRow


def validate_rows(rows: Sequence[RawRow], tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> list[RawRow]:
    """Positive closes only, one row per (date, ticker), sorted by (date, ticker)."""
    wanted = None if tickers is None else set(tickers)
    latest: dict[tuple[str, str], RawRow] = {}
    for row in rows:
        if row.close is not None and row.close > 0 and (wanted is None or row.ticker in wanted):
            latest[(row.date, row.ticker)] = row  # a later duplicate replaces an earlier one
    missing = sorted((wanted or set()) - {ticker for _, ticker in latest})
    if missing:
        raise KeyError(f"no usable row for ticker(s) {', '.join(missing)}")
    return [latest[key] for key in sorted(latest)]

    assert validate_rows(rows, ["B"]) == [RawRow("2024-01-02", "B", 2.0)]


def test_zero_and_negative_closes_are_dropped():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 0.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-03", "A", -2.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-04", "A", 1e-9),
    ]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [RawRow("2024-01-04", "A", 1e-9)]


def test_later_duplicate_wins_even_after_an_unusable_one():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", None),
    ]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 2.0)]


def test_key_error_names_every_missing_ticker():
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="B, C"):
        validate_rows([RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 1.0)], ["C", "A", "B"])


def test_no_rows_and_no_tickers_is_empty():
    assert validate_rows([]) == []

### mdata.align_calendar: One calendar
`def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices`
- **Invariants:** starts on the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close
- **Errors:** `GapError` naming the ticker when more than `max_gap` dates in a row are missing
- **Used by:** PL-01

`@dataclass(frozen=True) class RawRow: date: str; ticker: str; close: float | None`
- **Invariants:** `date` is ISO `yyyy-mm-dd`; `close` is None for a blank cell
- **Used by:** PL-01

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:13 -->
### mdata.Prices: Aligned daily closes
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/mdata.Prices/sample.json` · **Cases:** two tickers over six aligned dates
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Prices: dates: tuple[str, ...]; tickers: tuple[str, ...]; closes: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]`
- **Invariants:** dates ascending; tickers sorted; every ticker has one positive close per date
- **Used by:** PL-01, PL-02

### TC-03: A six-date gap
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** DATA-04 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/mdata/TC-03/
**Source:** B missing six dates in a row
**Expected:** `GapError` naming B · **Tolerance:** exact

<!-- docs/spec.md:16 -->
- **DATA-03** Every ticker sits on one calendar.
  - Given tickers that start on different dates and miss some dates, when aligned, then the calendar is the dates any ticker trades from the first date on which every ticker has a close, and a missing date takes that ticker's previous close.

<!-- docs/spec.md:18 -->
- **DATA-04** A long gap is an error, not a fill.
  - Given a ticker missing more than 5 calendar dates in a row, when aligned, then a `GapError` names the ticker.

D252Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/mdata/test_calendar.py (39 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/mdata/test_calendar.py
"""DATA-03 one calendar with filled gaps (TC-02); DATA-04 a long gap is an error (TC-03)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

import mdata
from mdata import RawRow, align_calendar

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def _rows(given: dict) -> list[RawRow]:
    return [RawRow(date, ticker, close) for date, ticker, close in given["rows"]]


def test_tc02_one_calendar_with_filled_gaps(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-02")
    prices = align_calendar(_rows(given), max_gap=given["max_gap"])
    assert list(prices.dates) == expected["dates"]
    assert list(prices.tickers) == expected["tickers"]
    assert {t: list(c) for t, c in prices.closes.items()} == expected["closes"]


def test_tc03_six_date_gap_is_gap_error(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-03")
    error = getattr(mdata, expected["raises"])
    with pytest.raises(error, match=expected["match"]):
        align_calendar(_rows(given), max_gap=given["max_gap"])


def test_a_gap_of_exactly_max_gap_dates_is_filled():
    dates = [f"2024-02-{d:02d}" for d in range(1, 9)]
    rows = [RawRow(d, "A", float(i + 1)) for i, d in enumerate(dates)]
    rows += [RawRow(dates[0], "B", 50.0), RawRow(dates[-1], "B", 55.0)]
    prices = align_calendar(rows, max_gap=6)
    assert prices.closes["B"] == (50.0,) * 7 + (55.0,)
    with pytest.raises(mdata.GapError, match="B"):
        align_calendar(rows, max_gap=5)

D258Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py (41 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py
"""PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar."""

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import GapError, Prices, RawRow


def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices:
    """Prices from the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close."""
    closes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    for row in rows:
        if row.close is not None:
            closes.setdefault(row.ticker, {})[row.date] = row.close
    if not closes:
        return Prices(dates=(), tickers=(), closes={})
    start = max(min(by_date) for by_date in closes.values())
    dates = tuple(sorted({d for by_date in closes.values() for d in by_date if d >= start}))
    tickers = tuple(sorted(closes))
    return Prices(
        dates=dates,
        tickers=tickers,
        closes={t: _filled(t, closes[t], dates, max_gap) for t in tickers},
    )


def _filled(
    ticker: str, by_date: dict[str, float], dates: tuple[str, ...], max_gap: int
) -> tuple[float, ...]:
    """The ticker's close on each date, a missing date taking the previous close."""
    out: list[float] = []
    missing = 0
    for day in dates:
        if day in by_date:
            out.append(by_date[day])
            missing = 0
            continue
        missing += 1
        if missing > max_gap:
            raise GapError(f"{ticker}: more than {max_gap} dates in a row missing before {day}")
        out.append(out[-1])
    return tuple(out)

def test_fills_a_missing_date_with_the_previous_close():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "B", 20.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 10.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-03", "A", 11.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-04", "A", 12.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-04", "B", 22.0),
    ]
    prices = align_calendar(rows)
    assert prices.dates == ("2024-01-02", "2024-01-03", "2024-01-04")
    assert prices.tickers == ("A", "B")
    assert prices.closes == {"A": (10.0, 11.0, 12.0), "B": (20.0, 20.0, 22.0)}


def test_starts_on_the_first_date_every_ticker_has():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-01-02", "A", 10.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-03", "A", 11.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-04", "B", 30.0),
        RawRow("2024-01-05", "A", 12.0),
    ]
    prices = align_calendar(rows)
    assert prices.dates == ("2024-01-04", "2024-01-05")
    assert prices.closes == {"A": (11.0, 12.0), "B": (30.0, 30.0)}


def test_gap_error_names_the_ticker_past_max_gap():
    rows = [RawRow(f"2024-01-0{d}", "A", 1.0) for d in range(2, 6)]
    rows += [RawRow("2024-01-02", "B", 2.0)]
    assert align_calendar(rows, max_gap=3).closes["B"] == (2.0,) * 4
    with pytest.raises(GapError, match="B"):
        align_calendar(rows, max_gap=2)

D265sayThe new unit test caught a real bug: a ticker with no close on the start date should take its earlier close. Fixing it so the fill walks from each ticker's own first date.
full text
The new unit test caught a real bug: a ticker with no close on the start date should take its earlier close. Fixing it so the fill walks from each ticker's own first date.
D266Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py (42 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py
"""PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar."""

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import GapError, Prices, RawRow


def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices:
    """Prices from the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close."""
    closes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    for row in rows:
        if row.close is not None:
            closes.setdefault(row.ticker, {})[row.date] = row.close
    if not closes:
        return Prices(dates=(), tickers=(), closes={})
    every_date = sorted({d for by_date in closes.values() for d in by_date})
    start = max(min(by_date) for by_date in closes.values())
    dates = tuple(d for d in every_date if d >= start)
    tickers = tuple(sorted(closes))
    return Prices(
        dates=dates,
        tickers=tickers,
        closes={t: _filled(t, closes[t], every_date, max_gap)[-len(dates) :] for t in tickers},
    )


def _filled(
    ticker: str, by_date: dict[str, float], every_date: list[str], max_gap: int
) -> tuple[float, ...]:
    """The ticker's close on each date from its first, a missing date taking the previous close."""
    out: list[float] = []
    missing = 0
    for day in every_date[every_date.index(min(by_date)) :]:
        if day in by_date:
            out.append(by_date[day])
            missing = 0
            continue
        missing += 1
        if missing > max_gap:
            raise GapError(f"{ticker}: more than {max_gap} dates in a row missing before {day}")
        out.append(out[-1])
    return tuple(out)

"""RISK-01: daily returns are log returns (TC-04)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import Prices
from risk import log_returns

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def test_tc04_geometric_closes(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-04")
    raw = given["prices"]
    prices = Prices(
        dates=tuple(raw["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(raw["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c) for t, c in raw["closes"].items()},
    )
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    assert returns.base_date == expected["base_date"]
    assert list(returns.dates) == expected["dates"]
    assert list(returns.tickers) == expected["tickers"]
    for ticker, values in expected["values"].items():
        assert list(returns.values[ticker]) == pytest.approx(values, abs=expected["tol"], rel=0)

<!-- docs/packages/risk/tests.md:16 -->
### TC-05: Rolling volatility from earlier returns
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** RISK-02 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/risk/TC-05/
**Source:** eight hand-made returns, window 4; sample standard deviation of the four returns before each date
**Expected:** no value for the first four dates, then the listed values · **Tolerance:** 1e-12 absolute

<!-- docs/spec.md:27 -->
- **RISK-02** Rolling volatility uses only earlier returns.
  - Given a window of w, when the volatility for date t is computed, then it is the sample standard deviation (n − 1) of the w returns before t, never t's own return, and there is no value until w earlier returns exist.

<!-- docs/references.md:5 -->
### RF-01: Volatility notebook
**File:** `references/vol_notebook.py` · **Hash:** 1e1d95d0 · **Kind:** code · **Milestones:** M1
- **Purpose:** the user's pandas notebook for returns, rolling volatility, annualizing, a summary and drawdown
- **Key parts:** `daily_returns`, `rolling_vol(returns, window)`, `annualize`, `summary`, `max_drawdown`
- **In → Out:** dates × tickers closes → returns, rolling vol, a summary frame, drawdown per ticker
- **Use for:** PL-02 `rolling_vol` and `annualize`, PL-03 `summary_stats` and `max_drawdown` design
- **Caveats:** pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

"""RISK-02: rolling volatility uses only earlier returns (TC-05)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, rolling_vol

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def _returns(raw: dict) -> Returns:
    return Returns(
        base_date=raw["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(raw["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(raw["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in raw["values"].items()},
    )


def test_tc05_rolling_volatility_from_earlier_returns(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-05")
    vol = rolling_vol(_returns(given["returns"]), given["window"])
    assert vol.window == expected["window"]
    assert vol.annualized is expected["annualized"]
    for ticker, values in expected["values"].items():
        got = vol.values[ticker]
        assert [v is None for v in got] == [v is None for v in values]
        assert [v for v in got if v is not None] == pytest.approx(
            [v for v in values if v is not None], abs=expected["tol"], rel=0
        )


@pytest.mark.parametrize("window", [1, 0, -3])
def test_window_below_two_is_value_error(golden, window):
    given, _ = golden("risk", "TC-05")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="window"):
        rolling_vol(_returns(given["returns"]), window)

"""PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility."""

from statistics import stdev

from risk.types import Returns, VolSeries


def rolling_vol(returns: Returns, window: int) -> VolSeries:
    """Sample stdev of the `window` returns before each date (RISK-02)."""
    if window < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"window must be at least 2, got {window}")
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=window,
        annualized=False,
        values={
            t: tuple(stdev(r[i - window : i]) if i >= window else None for i in range(len(r)))
            for t, r in returns.values.items()
        },
    )

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

D305Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_annualize.py (49 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_annualize.py
"""RISK-03: volatility is annualized over trading days (TC-06)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import dataclasses

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, annualize

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def _vol(raw: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = raw["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(r["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=raw["window"],
        annualized=raw["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in raw["values"].items()},
    )


def test_tc06_annualizing(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-06")
    vol = _vol(given["vol"])
    out = annualize(vol)
    assert out.annualized is expected["annualized"]
    assert out.returns == vol.returns
    assert out.window == vol.window
    for ticker, values in expected["values"].items():
        got = out.values[ticker]
        assert [v is None for v in got] == [v is None for v in values]
        assert [v for v in got if v is not None] == pytest.approx(
            [v for v in values if v is not None], abs=expected["tol"], rel=0
        )


def test_annualizing_an_annualized_series_is_value_error(golden):

    vol = dataclasses.replace(_vol(given["vol"]), annualized=True)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="annualized"):

D310Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/annualize.py (21 lines)

"""PL-02 step 4: annualize volatility."""

import math
from dataclasses import replace

from risk.types import VolSeries


def annualize(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> VolSeries:
    """Each daily volatility times the square root of `periods_per_year` (RISK-03)."""
    if vol.annualized:
        raise ValueError("the volatility series is already annualized")
    scale = math.sqrt(periods_per_year)
    return replace(
        vol,
        annualized=True,
        values={
            t: tuple(None if v is None else v * scale for v in values)
            for t, values in vol.values.items()
        },
    )

"""annualize: daily volatility scaled by the square root of the periods per year."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, annualize


def _vol(data: dict, *, annualized: bool) -> VolSeries:
    raw = data["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=raw["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(raw["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(raw["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in raw["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=data["window"],
        annualized=annualized,
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in data["values"].items()},
    )


def test_sample_comes_back_annualized_and_aligned(contract_sample):
    vol = _vol(contract_sample("risk.VolSeries"), annualized=False)
    out = annualize(vol)
    assert isinstance(out, VolSeries)
    assert out.annualized
    assert out.returns == vol.returns
    assert out.window == vol.window
    for ticker in vol.returns.tickers:
        assert len(out.values[ticker]) == len(vol.returns.dates)
        assert [v is None for v in out.values[ticker]] == [v is None for v in vol.values[ticker]]


def _daily(values: tuple[float | None, ...]) -> VolSeries:
    dates = tuple(f"d{i}" for i in range(1, len(values) + 1))
    returns = Returns(
        base_date="d0", dates=dates, tickers=("A",), values={"A": (0.0,) * len(values)}
    )
    return VolSeries(returns=returns, window=2, annualized=False, values={"A": values})


def test_scales_by_root_252_and_keeps_gaps():
    assert annualize(_daily((None, 0.01, 0.0))).values["A"] == (
        None,
        pytest.approx(0.15874507866387544),
        0.0,
    )


def test_periods_per_year_sets_the_scale():
    assert annualize(_daily((0.5,)), periods_per_year=4).values["A"] == (1.0,)


def test_annualized_input_is_value_error(contract_sample):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="already annualized"):
        annualize(_vol(contract_sample("risk.VolSeries"), annualized=True))

<!-- docs/spec.md:31 -->
- **RISK-04** Each ticker gets a whole-period summary.
  - Given returns, when summarized, then the annualized return is 252 × the mean, the annualized volatility is √252 × the sample standard deviation, the Sharpe ratio is their quotient (0 when the volatility is 0; no risk-free rate), and the last rolling volatility is reported.

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

D321Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_summary.py (37 lines)

"""RISK-04: each ticker gets a whole-period summary (TC-07)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, summary_stats

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def _vol(raw: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = raw["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(r["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=raw["window"],
        annualized=raw["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in raw["values"].items()},
    )


def test_tc07_whole_period_summary(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-07")
    summary = summary_stats(_vol(given["vol"]))
    tol = expected["tol"]
    assert [s.ticker for s in summary] == [e["ticker"] for e in expected["summary"]]
    for got, want in zip(summary, expected["summary"], strict=True):
        assert got.ann_return == pytest.approx(want["ann_return"], abs=tol, rel=0)
        assert got.ann_vol == pytest.approx(want["ann_vol"], abs=tol, rel=0)
        assert got.sharpe == pytest.approx(want["sharpe"], abs=tol, rel=0)
        assert got.last_vol == pytest.approx(want["last_vol"], abs=tol, rel=0)

D326Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py (29 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py
"""PL-03 step 2: whole-period summary per ticker."""

import math
from statistics import mean, stdev

from risk.types import TickerSummary, VolSeries


def summary_stats(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> tuple[TickerSummary, ...]:
    """Annualized return, volatility, Sharpe and last rolling volatility (RISK-04)."""
    returns = vol.returns
    if len(returns.dates) < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"a summary needs at least two returns, got {len(returns.dates)}")
    out = []
    for ticker in returns.tickers:
        values = returns.values[ticker]
        ann_return = periods_per_year * mean(values)
        ann_vol = math.sqrt(periods_per_year) * stdev(values)
        rolling = [v for v in vol.values[ticker] if v is not None]
        out.append(
            TickerSummary(
                ticker=ticker,
                ann_return=ann_return,
                ann_vol=ann_vol,
                sharpe=ann_return / ann_vol if ann_vol else 0.0,
                last_vol=rolling[-1] if rolling else None,
            )
        )
    return tuple(out)

<!-- docs/packages/risk/contract.md:24 -->
### risk.Drawdown: Worst fall from a peak
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/risk.Drawdown/sample.json` · **Cases:** one ticker's fall from its peak to its trough
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Drawdown: ticker: str; depth: float; peak_date: str; trough_date: str`
- **Invariants:** `depth` ≤ 0; `peak_date` ≤ `trough_date`; with no fall, both are the returns' `base_date`
- **Used by:** PL-03

<!-- docs/packages/risk/tests.md:31 -->
### TC-08: Drawdown with dates
**Pipeline:** PL-03 · **Covers:** RISK-05 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/risk/TC-08/
**Source:** closes 100, 110, 99, 88, 95, 120 (X), a rising series (Y), a first-day fall (Z)
**Expected:** X −20% from the second date to the fourth; Y 0 at the first date; Z peaks at the first date · **Tolerance:** 1e-12 absolute on depth, dates exact

<!-- docs/spec.md:33 -->
- **RISK-05** Maximum drawdown comes with its dates.
  - Given closes, when the drawdown is computed, then wealth starts at 1 on the first date, and the result is the deepest fall below the running peak (trough / peak − 1, at most 0) with the date of that peak and the date of the trough; the first if tied. With no fall, both dates are the first date.

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

D338Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_drawdown.py (26 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_drawdown.py
"""RISK-05: maximum drawdown comes with its dates (TC-08)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, max_drawdown

pytestmark = pytest.mark.intent


def test_tc08_drawdown_with_dates(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-08")
    raw = given["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=raw["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(raw["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(raw["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in raw["values"].items()},
    )
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(returns)
    assert [d.ticker for d in drawdowns] == [e["ticker"] for e in expected["drawdowns"]]
    for got, want in zip(drawdowns, expected["drawdowns"], strict=True):
        assert got.depth == pytest.approx(want["depth"], abs=expected["tol"], rel=0)
        assert got.peak_date == want["peak_date"]
        assert got.trough_date == want["trough_date"]

D343Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/drawdown.py (28 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r1/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/drawdown.py
"""PL-03 step 3: maximum drawdown per ticker."""

import math
from itertools import accumulate

from risk.types import Drawdown, Returns


def max_drawdown(returns: Returns) -> tuple[Drawdown, ...]:
    """The deepest fall below the running peak, with its dates (RISK-05)."""
    dates = (returns.base_date, *returns.dates)
    return tuple(_worst_fall(t, dates, returns.values[t]) for t in returns.tickers)


def _worst_fall(ticker: str, dates: tuple[str, ...], values: tuple[float, ...]) -> Drawdown:
    """Wealth starts at 1 on the first date; the first deepest fall wins a tie."""
    wealth = [1.0, *(math.exp(s) for s in accumulate(values))]
    peak = 0
    worst = Drawdown(ticker=ticker, depth=0.0, peak_date=dates[0], trough_date=dates[0])
    for i, level in enumerate(wealth):
        if level > wealth[peak]:
            peak = i
        depth = level / wealth[peak] - 1
        if depth < worst.depth:
            worst = Drawdown(
                ticker=ticker, depth=depth, peak_date=dates[peak], trough_date=dates[i]
            )
    return worst

def test_no_fall_is_zero_depth_on_the_base_date():
    returns = Returns(base_date="d0", dates=("d1", "d2"), tickers=("A",), values={"A": (0.1, 0.0)})
    (drawdown,) = max_drawdown(returns)

def test_a_tie_keeps_the_first_fall():
    half, double = math.log(0.5), math.log(2.0)
    returns = Returns(
        base_date="d0",
        dates=("d1", "d2", "d3"),
        tickers=("A",),
        values={"A": (half, double, half)},
    )
    (drawdown,) = max_drawdown(returns)
    assert drawdown.depth == pytest.approx(-0.5)
    assert (drawdown.peak_date, drawdown.trough_date) == ("d0", "d1")

"""System: risk_report runs PL-03 -> PL-02 -> PL-01 on a real CSV, with no fakes."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import pytest

import mdata
import risk
from risk import Report, risk_report

ROW_KEYS = [
    "ticker",
    "ann_return",
    "ann_vol",
    "sharpe",
    "last_vol",
    "max_drawdown",
    "peak_date",
    "trough_date",
]


def _csv_from_prices(prices: dict, path) -> None:
    """The mdata.Prices sample as a messy CSV: shuffled, with a blank, a zero and a duplicate."""
    lines = ["date,ticker,close"]
    for ticker in reversed(prices["tickers"]):
        for date, close in reversed(list(zip(prices["dates"], prices["closes"][ticker], strict=True))):
            lines.append(f"{date},{ticker},{close!r}")
    first = prices["dates"][0]
    lines.insert(1, f"{first},{prices['tickers'][0]},")
    lines.insert(2, f"{first},{prices['tickers'][0]},0")
    lines.insert(1, f"{first},{prices['tickers'][1]},999.0")  # overridden by the later row
    path.write_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n")


@pytest.fixture
def prices_csv(tmp_path, contract_sample):
    path = tmp_path / "prices.csv"
    _csv_from_prices(contract_sample("mdata.Prices"), path)
    return path


def test_real_load_prices_agrees_with_its_fake(prices_csv, contract_fake):
    fake = contract_fake("mdata.load_prices").load_prices(prices_csv)
    assert mdata.load_prices(prices_csv) == fake


def test_real_vol_series_agrees_with_its_fake(prices_csv, contract_fake):
    fake = contract_fake("risk.vol_series").vol_series(prices_csv, window=2)
    real = risk.vol_series(prices_csv, window=2)
    assert real.returns.base_date == fake.returns.base_date
    assert real.returns.dates == fake.returns.dates
    assert real.annualized is fake.annualized is True
    for ticker in fake.returns.tickers:
        assert real.returns.values[ticker] == pytest.approx(fake.returns.values[ticker], abs=1e-12)
        got, want = real.values[ticker], fake.values[ticker]
        assert [v is None for v in got] == [v is None for v in want]
        assert [v for v in got if v is not None] == pytest.approx(
            [v for v in want if v is not None], abs=1e-12
        )


def test_risk_report_writes_the_report(prices_csv, tmp_path, contract_sample):
    out = tmp_path / "report.json"
    report = risk_report(prices_csv, out, window=2)
    assert isinstance(report, Report)
    written = json.loads(out.read_text())
    assert list(written) == ["tickers"]
    assert written["tickers"] == [dict(row) for row in report.rows]
    expected = contract_sample("risk.Report")["rows"]
    assert [row["ticker"] for row in report.rows] == [row["ticker"] for row in expected]
    for got, want in zip(report.rows, expected, strict=True):
        assert list(got) == ROW_KEYS
        for key in ROW_KEYS[1:]:
            if isinstance(want[key], str):
                assert got[key] == want[key]
            else:
                assert got[key] == pytest.approx(want[key], abs=1e-9)


def test_risk_report_is_deterministic(prices_csv, tmp_path):
    first, second = tmp_path / "first.json", tmp_path / "second.json"
    risk_report(prices_csv, first, window=2)
    risk_report(prices_csv, second, window=2)
    assert first.read_bytes() == second.read_bytes()


def test_risk_report_selects_tickers(prices_csv, tmp_path):
    report = risk_report(prices_csv, tmp_path / "report.json", window=2, tickers=["BBB"])
    assert [row["ticker"] for row in report.rows] == ["BBB"]

"""risk_report on a real CSV: the errors of the pipelines underneath reach the caller."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import GapError
from risk import risk_report

CSV = """date,ticker,close
2024-01-02,A,10.0
2024-01-02,B,20.0
2024-01-03,A,10.5
2024-01-03,B,20.4
2024-01-04,A,10.1
2024-01-04,B,20.1
2024-01-05,A,10.9
2024-01-05,B,20.8
"""


@pytest.fixture
def csv_path(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "prices.csv"
    path.write_text(CSV)
    return path


def test_window_below_two_is_value_error(csv_path, tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="window"):
        risk_report(csv_path, tmp_path / "report.json", window=1)


def test_unknown_ticker_is_key_error(csv_path, tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="Z"):
        risk_report(csv_path, tmp_path / "report.json", window=2, tickers=["Z"])


def test_long_gap_is_gap_error(tmp_path):
    rows = [f"2024-01-{d:02d},A,10.0" for d in range(1, 10)] + ["2024-01-01,B,5.0"]
    path = tmp_path / "gappy.csv"
    path.write_text("date,ticker,close\n" + "\n".join(rows) + "\n")
    with pytest.raises(GapError, match="B"):
        risk_report(path, tmp_path / "report.json", window=2)


def test_malformed_file_is_value_error(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "bad.csv"
    path.write_text("day,ticker,close\n2024-01-02,A,1\n")
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="date"):
        risk_report(path, tmp_path / "report.json", window=2)


def test_report_path_is_the_out_path(csv_path, tmp_path):
    out = tmp_path / "nested.json"
    assert risk_report(csv_path, str(out), window=2).path == str(out)
    assert out.is_file()

full text
All 21 M1 tasks are ticked and committed on `build/M1`, but the milestone exit check, `scripts/check.sh full --exit`, still fails on one finding. I haven't merged anything.

**What's built**
- `risk.risk_report` turns a prices CSV into the JSON report by running PL-03 → PL-02 → PL-01 with no fakes. The fakes are swapped in only by the smoke tests, and the contract tests check them.
- All 100 tests pass with `pytest tests`: the three smoke tests, TC-01–TC-08, the contract tests, the unit tests, and new system tests in `tests/system/test_e2e.py`.
- The system tests check three more things:
  - The real `mdata.load_prices` and `risk.vol_series` give the same results as their fakes on the same sample.
  - The report matches the contract's sample report.
  - Two runs of the report write identical files (QR-01).
- `full --exit` passes on everything else: no stubs left, 100% branch coverage, no duplicate code, pip-audit clean, and `dt layout` shows all three pipelines in step order.

**Changes from the plan**
- **Contract revision:** `risk.Drawdown` gained `trough_date` before anything was frozen, with your approval; that's commit `2e5dd9d`.
- **Drawdown tie-break:** RISK-05's "first if tied" decides ties. The deepest fall wins, and between equal falls the earliest one is kept.
- **Gap counting (M1-T15):** a forward fill now starts from each ticker's own first date and counts missing dates on the calendar of every ticker. Without this, a ticker with no close on the first shared date would have nothing to fill from, and one of my unit tests caught exactly that.
- **`last_vol`:** it is the last rolling value that exists, not the last entry. TC-07 requires this, because X's final rolling value is empty but its expected `last_vol` is 0.18.
- **Reference notebook:** I didn't use RF-01's formulas. They include the date's own return, use population standard deviation and annualize over 365 days, and the spec says otherwise on all three.

**Decision for you: M1-R01** (recorded under the exit checkpoint as Important, committed in `6cbe4a5`)
vulture reports `TickerSummary.sharpe` and `.last_vol` in `risk/types.py` as unused, and that alone fails `full --exit` (CR-41). They are used, but only through `dataclasses.asdict` in `report.py::write_report`. That file belongs to M1-T10, so fixing it wasn't within M1-T21's Writes. The options:
1. **A small fix task (my recommendation):** `write_report` builds each row field by field. That's more explicit and needs no exception.
2. **A whitelist entry:** add the two names to `scripts/vulture_whitelist.py` through build-setup. That file is protected, and CR-41 says the count must not grow.

**Not done:** I didn't run the reviewer passes at the checkpoints (after M1-T01, M1-T10, M1-T20 and at exit), because this arm had no subagents. I ran `check.sh full` by hand after M1-T01 and `full --exit` at the end; `dt build status` still lists those reviews as ready.

Which fix do you want for M1-R01? Once `full --exit` passes, should I merge `build/M1`, or would you like the checkpoint reviews run first?
